## M-099 Path jail — pure layer DONE (2026-11-06 keeper tick)
- Base: team-05 freshly reset to banked base f9df631 = M-098 closure (98/272). Built ONLY the pure layer in team-05; no push, no README touched, no config_defaults / config_catalog / PUBLIC_API / OWNERSHIP / CAMPAIGN_STATE / roadmap files touched.
- Existing confinement found (composition, not duplication): mythic_agent/core/workspace.py already ships the elder single-root road — resolve_file / assert_within_workspace / canonical_workspace_root resolve symlinks with Path.resolve and raise SecurityError on escape, and the live file tools ride it today (agents/tools.py resolves reads/writes through resolve_file; core/validation.py checks file-tool args against the workspace the same way). That road knows exactly one root, answers by raising, and carries write-specific refusals of its own (workspace root itself + .git on writes) — it has no named grants, no allow/deny decision as data, no stored gate, no /pathjail grammar. core/permission_profiles.py (M-095) binds paths only as a coarse read/write posture word; core/policy.py ToolPolicy authorizes by tool name/mode with no path binding at all; core/sandbox.py guards commands and core/os_sandbox.py (M-097) plans command confinement — neither judges file-tool paths. M-099 was therefore built as the missing judge composing with the elder road, not replacing it: the jail is the confinement decision, the elder resolve_file road keeps its own write rules.
- Built: mythic_agent/core/path_jail.py — validated grants (one grant = one absolute directory path, realpath-normalized; a relative grant refuses; duplicates dropped; one malformed grant refuses the whole list; grants are directory prefixes — file-vs-directory on disk is data, the prefix is what is judged) + check_path/decide_path, one mechanical judge: relative candidates resolve against the workspace, then os.path.realpath resolves the whole candidate BEFORE judging; allow iff the resolved path is the workspace root itself or inside it, or a grant root itself or inside it, with inside judged on path components via os.path.commonpath (the /ws2-vs-/ws sibling-prefix trick never matches); default deny fail-closed; malformed candidates (empty, non-text, NUL-bearing) are DENIED as decisions while malformed workspace/grant data raises (stored-data rule) + JailDecision as data (to_record type path_jail_decision) + JailRefusal shaped as news exactly like the network gates (type path_jail_refusal, news true, surface session_event, plain headline) — included because a file tool reaching outside the jail is an escape attempt with the same news value as a refused network reach + PathJail dataclass holding only the grants (the workspace root is the session's project root, supplied per check as jail.check(path, workspace_root)) + gate readers (enabled_from_config ships on / non-bool raises, grants_from_config absent = no grants, jail_from_config, set_enabled + GateChange with event type path_jail_setting_changed) + shared /pathjail parser (show/on/off/grants/check <path>/grant <abs-path>, grant validated in the parser) and formatters (format_path_jail renders PLAIN_EXPLANATION + setting line + resolved workspace + grants + a sample allowed check, format_grants, format_jail_decision, format_refusal, format_gate_change: "The path jail is now on/off."). The module judges only: no file opened, written or read, no setting mutated.
- Symlink-resolution honesty note: symlinks are RESOLVED (os.path.realpath) before judging, never assumed away — a symlinked directory inside the workspace pointing outside is denied for paths through it, and a symlinked directory outside pointing in is judged by its resolved path. For a candidate that does not exist yet, realpath resolves the deepest existing ancestor and the remaining tail is judged lexically beneath it; that is stated in the module docstring, and it is safe for the honest reason that a tail that does not exist cannot itself be a symlink — the moment any part of it exists as a link, resolution follows it (pinned by the nonexistent-tail-through-a-symlink-escape test).
- Verification on the laptop: tests/test_path_jail.py 35 passed (pure, TIER: integration, REAL directories + REAL symlinks in tmp_path); neighbours tests/test_network_gates.py + tests/test_permission_profiles.py + tests/test_os_sandbox.py 155 passed combined.
- Owed coordinator deltas at banking: catalogue S-91 for the new path_jail setting + S-92 for path_grants (next free ids after S-89 network_gates / S-90 network_allowlist), config_defaults lines path_jail: true + path_grants: [], PUBLIC_API + OWNERSHIP regen, roadmap flip M-099 to done (99/272 at banking), ledger row; dead-code quarantine entries if the banking gate flags the new Agent-facing surface (M-074 pattern).
- Still owed before M-099 can bank: agent layer (Agent path-jail status/set/grant methods, journal-first with save-failure revert; the live file-tool roads judged through the jail — check_path asked before a file tool touches a path, denials surfaced through the path_jail_refusal record as session news; grants stored as plain path strings under path_grants) + /pathjail wiring on terminal and TUI roads + chat_screen help/usage/routing; then coordinator banking in roadmap order (merge → full suite → regen → closure → explicit-refspec push → ls-remote verify).

- Built: mythic_agent/core/plan_mode.py — plan_mode gate reader (setting plan_mode, ships OFF / false by default because building is the normal posture; a non-bool stored value raises, never guessed at) + set_enabled / GateChange (event plan_mode_setting_changed) + decide(plan_mode_on, effect), one mechanical judgement: effects are data (read / write / execute, with the sibling tool-kind words path_read / path_write / edit / command / run read as the effects they name; an unknown effect raises) — in plan posture read is allowed and write / execute are denied, out of plan posture everything is allowed + the Plan document in Part IX form (Plan: title + intent + an ordered tuple of PlanSlice; PlanSlice: title + intent + acceptance_checks; frozen dataclasses that validate on construction — at least one slice, every slice title / intent non-empty, every slice carries at least one non-empty acceptance check — plus validate_plan / validate_plan_slice / build_plan for mapping data, unknown keys refuse, approved flag must be a bool and defaults false) + the approval gate (approve(plan) returns an approved copy via replace, the original stays unapproved and approval cannot leak into another plan; can_build(plan_mode_on, plan): out of posture always true, in posture true only for an approved plan) + PlanRefusal shaped as news exactly like the path jail / network gates (type plan_mode_refusal, news true, surface session_event, plain headline) with build_refusal / refusal_for_effect / refusal_for_build / refusal_record / format_refusal + shared /plan parser (show / on / off / approve / build / check <effect>, the effect validated in the parser, anything else is the USAGE error) and formatters (format_plan_status is the status line's one sentence; format_plan renders one whole plan in order with its checks; format_plan_mode renders the whole slice; format_gate_change: "Plan mode is now on/off."). The module judges and formats only: no file written, no setting mutated, nothing run, no agent / TUI imports.

## M-100 agent layer DONE (2026-11-06 keeper tick)
- Built in team-06 on the M-100 pure layer (b9d43a1, on banked base d893465 = M-099 closure, 99/272), following the M-099/M-098 sibling pattern exactly: Agent plan_mode_status (enabled + held plan as its plain record or None + format_plan_mode screen + PLAIN_EXPLANATION + can_build; malformed stored config raises) / set_plan_mode (journal-first via pure set_enabled, plain bool under plan_mode, event plan_mode_setting_changed, save failure restores the previous config exactly + plan_mode_setting_reverted compensating event + RuntimeError) / the current plan held in the agent layer alone (session state, never a stored setting): set_current_plan built through validate_plan (one malformed piece refuses the whole plan and the previously held plan stays held), build_current_plan built through build_plan, approve_current_plan approved through approve (the held plan is replaced by its approved copy, so approval belongs to that plan alone; no plan held refuses plainly) / check_plan_effect (decide asked before a tool effect; the decision is computed always - an unknown effect raises - and with the gate on a denied effect publishes its plan_mode_refusal record as a session event, news, not a log line) / check_plan_build (can_build asked before building while the gate is on; a refused build publishes its refusal record the same way) + /plan (show/on/off/approve/build/check <effect>) on the terminal road and the CommandHandler/TUI road sharing parse_plan_args and the pure formatters + chat_screen help line, usage entry, and system-command routing + the terminal /help line.
- Gate-off honesty note: decide itself is posture-shaped - with the gate off every effect is allowed and can_build is true, so a gate-off check computes its (allowing) decision and publishes nothing, exactly as specified (decision computed but unpublished); refusal-as-news exists only for gate-on denials, which is where a denial can occur.
- Honest boundary (live tool road): check_plan_effect / check_plan_build are the judgement + session-news roads, exactly like M-099 check_path_jail and M-098 check_network_reach (which decide and publish, and intercept nothing themselves). Wiring every live tool call site through check_plan_effect is beyond the sibling pattern and was NOT done and is NOT claimed - the decision and refusal-as-news are available to every surface through the agent methods and /plan check / /plan build.

## M-101 Hooks - the full lifecycle - pure layer DONE (2026-11-06 keeper tick)

- Built: mythic_agent/core/hooks.py - HOOK_EVENTS, the seven events (pre_tool, post_tool, pre_edit, post_edit, on_test, on_commit, on_session; event words case-insensitive, dashes as underscores, unknown raises) + Hook frozen dataclass validating on construction (event, non-empty command, name default empty, enabled bool default True, timeout_seconds int 1..600 default 30 with bool refused; to_record) + validate_hook/build_hook from a mapping (command or script, timeout_seconds or timeout; conflicting spellings and unknown keys refuse) + validate_hooks/build_hooks for a list (one bad hook refuses all; None -> ()) + hooks_from_config reading SETTING_KEY hooks (missing -> ()) + select_hooks (enabled hooks for one event, in listed order) + parse_hook_output (a JSON record stating decision block, or block: true, with its reason from reason/message/detail; stdout read before stderr; no record -> ("", "")) + evaluate_result -> HookVerdict (event, hook label, decision allow/block/error, reason, exit_code; .blocks; to_record type hook_verdict): exit 0 allows, exit 2 blocks with the reason from stderr then stdout with the exact fallback "the hook blocked giving no reason", a JSON block blocks at any exit code with its reason, any other non-zero exit is an error that blocks for pre_tool/pre_edit/on_test/on_commit and is news-only (blocks False) for post_tool/post_edit/on_session; a non-int exit code raises + block_record ({"type": "hook_block", "news": True, event, hook, reason} when the verdict blocks, else None) + format_block / format_verdict / format_hooks (empty starts "Hooks: none configured.") / format_hook + parse_hooks_args -> (action, event-or-None): empty/list/show -> ("show", None), show <event> or a bare event word -> ("show", event), check <event> -> ("check", event), anything else raises the USAGE error. The module judges and formats only: no file written, no setting mutated, no script run.
- Verification on the laptop: tests/test_hooks.py 34 passed (pure, TIER: integration); neighbours tests/test_plan_mode.py + tests/test_network_gates.py + tests/test_path_jail.py 185 passed combined. No defects found in the new code (both suites green on their first full runs).

## M-101 agent layer DONE (2026-11-06 keeper tick)
- Built in team-07 on the M-101 pure layer (b813012, on banked base 4a437cb = M-100 closure, 100/272), following the M-100/M-099/M-098 sibling pattern: Agent hooks_status (configured hooks as plain records + format_hooks screen + PLAIN_EXPLANATION; malformed stored config raises, never silently narrowed) / configured_hooks / run_hooks(event, stdin_text="") - the live lifecycle road: select_hooks for the event, each selected hook's command run for real via subprocess (shell, cwd = the agent's workspace, the hook's own timeout_seconds as the bound), each finished run judged through evaluate_result alone, and every blocking verdict published through block_record as a hook_block session event (news, the reason the coder must answer); verdicts returned in run order / _run_one_hook (a timeout is judged an error at exit 124 with the timeout named in its reason; a command the OS cannot start is judged an error at exit 127 - neither dressed up as an allow) + /hooks (show, show <event>, check <event>) on the terminal road and the CommandHandler/TUI road sharing parse_hooks_args and the pure formatters (show <event> filters the configured list to that event and runs nothing; check <event> runs the event's enabled hooks and reports each verdict through format_verdict) + chat_screen help line, usage entry, and system-command routing + the terminal /help line.
- No gate was invented: hooks is a list setting with no enabled gate in the pure layer, so there is no set/on/off method - the list itself is the setting, exactly as the pure layer holds it. There is likewise no stored-list mutation road in the pure layer to mirror, so none was built; status + run/check are the layer.
- Post-event honesty: an error at post_tool/post_edit/on_session blocks nothing in evaluate_result's own judgement (blocks False), so run_hooks publishes nothing for it - it rides back in the returned verdicts and on the check surface as a news-only error, never as a block. Blocks (exit 2, JSON block at any exit, and errors before the act or at a gate) are the only verdicts published as session news.

## M-103 Background tasks — pure layer DONE (2026-11-06 keeper tick)
- Base: team-09 verified at banked base 9d05915 = M-102 closure (102/272). Built ONLY the pure layer in team-09; no push, no README touched, no config_defaults / config_catalog / PUBLIC_API / OWNERSHIP / CAMPAIGN_STATE / WAVE_PLAN / roadmap files touched.
- Existing basis found (composition, not duplication): the gap audit names mythic_agent/agents/tasks.py as this slice's basis — a typed task lifecycle with budgets and cancellation (a thread-safe registry recording transitions, states queued / running / completed / failed / cancelled) that executes nothing and knows nothing of a long command's output, live tail, or completion announced into the session; the feature census says managed background jobs with live tails are owed. M-103 was built as that missing job layer ON the typed lifecycle: the job states are the tasks layer's own TaskState words (pinned by a test asserting the two vocabularies are equal), so a job and a task are never described in two dialects. It does not replace the agent-task registry, which tracks sub-agent tasks, not commands.
- Command-surface name chosen: /background — the slice's own title word, in the one-word lowercase house style of /hookpacks, /largefile, /plan; no /tasks or /jobs surface existed in the tree, and /background names the feature the roadmap names (background tasks) without inventing a second noun for the same thing.
- Built: mythic_agent/core/background_tasks.py — JobSpec frozen dataclass validating on construction (argv as a list of non-empty text words — a single shell string refuses outright, so no spec can smuggle a command line to be eval'd later; optional cwd, optional label defaulting in reports to the command text itself) + validate_job_spec / build_job_spec for mapping data (argv or command, label or name; conflicting spellings and unknown keys refuse) + Job frozen dataclass (id, spec, state, exit code, kill request, kept tail lines, dropped-line count, tail bounds — validating on construction: a running job carries no exit code, a completed job's code is 0, a failed job's is not 0, a cancelled job carries none) + legal transitions only via transition_job / LEGAL_TRANSITIONS (queued -> running / cancelled; running -> completed / failed / cancelled; terminal states move nowhere) with start_job and finish_job (exit 0 completes, any other code fails — the mechanical reading the hooks layer gives a finished run; a non-integer exit code raises) + the bounded live tail as pure functions (append_output / append_output_text only on a running job; newest lines kept within a line bound of 200 and a byte bound of 65536, oldest pushed out and counted in dropped_lines, shown wherever the tail is shown; a single line is never cut — an over-long line is kept whole and the byte bound applies between lines) + job_tail + KillRequest as data (requested flag + reason; request_kill records the request and changes no state, a second request refuses, a finished job refuses; apply_kill lands a requested kill as cancelled and refuses without one; kill_job is request + land in one move; this layer signals no process) + completion as news (completion_record returns the background_job_completed record — type, news true, surface session_event, job id, label, state, exit_code and a one-line plain headline via format_completion — only for a terminal job, else None; format_completion raises for a job that has not finished) + BackgroundJobRegistry, an in-memory/on-disk-neutral registry model (create / get / list_jobs in creation order with an optional state filter / start / append_output / append_output_text / request_kill / kill / finish / completion_record; caller-supplied ids used as given, duplicates refuse, unknown ids raise KeyError as the tasks registry does; unsupplied ids are the deterministic sequence job-1, job-2, ... skipping numbers a supplied id already took — no randomness a test cannot pin) + PLAIN_EXPLANATION + shared /background parser (empty/list/show -> ("show", None); show <job> or a bare job id -> ("show", job); tail [<job>] -> ("tail", job-or-None); kill <job> -> ("kill", job); start <command...> -> ("start", the command words as one string for the agent layer to split into argv, never to run through a shell); a grammar word in the wrong shape is a USAGE error, never a job id) and formatters (format_job one line, format_jobs with the plain "Background jobs: none." empty statement, format_tail with the dropped count stated, format_kill_request, format_background the whole slice). The module holds jobs as data only: no process run, nothing signalled, no file written, no setting mutated, no agent / UI imports.
- Defects found and fixed during verification: (1) build_job and the registry refused a bare argv list — the natural way to name a command — because spec coercion only accepted a JobSpec or a mapping; a _coerce_spec helper now accepts a JobSpec, a mapping, or a bare argv list in build_job, Job construction and registry create, while validate_job_spec keeps its strict mapping-only refusal. (2) The parser read a bare "kill" as a job id to show; a grammar word (show / list / tail / kill / start) in the wrong shape now raises the USAGE error instead of falling through to the bare-id reading. Both were caught by the new suite's first run (22 failures, all from these two roots), fixed in the module, and the suite went green with no test weakened.

The two long-classified environment failures (test_context_compiler tiktoken estimator, test_public_api PortAudio) were not in these neighbour files and did not appear.

PUBLIC_API + OWNERSHIP regen, roadmap flip M-103 to done (103/272 at banking), ledger row; dead-code quarantine entries if the banking gate flags the new Agent-facing surface (M-074 pattern).

## M-104 The Keeper pattern built in — pure layer DONE (2026-11-06 keeper tick)
- Base: team-10 verified at banked base 16764f2 = M-103 closure (103/272). Built ONLY the pure layer in team-10; no push, no README touched, no config_defaults / config_catalog / PUBLIC_API / OWNERSHIP / CAMPAIGN_STATE / WAVE_PLAN / roadmap files touched.
- Existing basis found (composition, not duplication): the keeper law is Volmarr's own standing design, worked by hand over this campaign — a ten-minute minder that checks whether guarded work has stopped and continues it itself, never merely reporting. M-103 ships the watched work itself as data (managed background jobs with live tails and completion news) but nothing in the tree judges whether a piece of work has gone quiet too long or says what is owed when it has. M-104 was built as that missing judgement ON the M-103 sibling pattern, not replacing it: the same frozen validated dataclasses, build_/validate_ pairs with alternate spellings, PLAIN_EXPLANATION, USAGE, shared parser and formatters, deterministic-id registry, and *_record functions shaped as session news returning None when not applicable.
- Command-surface name chosen: /keeper — the slice's own title word, in the one-word lowercase house style of /background, /hookpacks, /plan; no /keeper surface existed in the tree.
- Built: mythic_agent/core/keeper.py — KEEPER_SLICE = "M-104" + WATCH_KINDS (background_job / crew_member / task; kind words read case-insensitively with dashes as underscores, unknown kinds refuse) naming WHAT is watched (a background job from M-103, a crew member's task, any tracked task) + WatchTarget frozen dataclass validating on construction (optional target_id — an empty one refuses; non-empty label; stall_after_seconds a whole number 60..86400, default 600 — the 10-minute keeper cadence — with booleans refused; last_activity_at an epoch float >= 0, default 0.0; restarts a count >= 0, default 0; max_restarts 0..100, default 3, booleans refused) + validate_watch_target / build_watch_target for mapping data (kind or type, label or name, stall_after_seconds or stall_after, last_activity_at or last_seen_at, restarts, max_restarts, target_id or id; conflicting spellings refuse, unknown keys refuse, one bad value refuses) + record_activity (a copy with the activity clock advanced; an earlier moment raises — time never runs backwards) + WATCH_STATUSES (active / quiet / stalled) with judge_watch returning a WatchVerdict frozen dataclass (target_id, kind, label, status, age_seconds, action): age is now minus last activity (a negative age raises), younger than half the bound is active, at or past half but under the bound is quiet, at or past the bound is stalled + ACTIONS (none / restart / report): active and quiet owe none, a stalled target is owed restart while restarts remain and report when they are exhausted — the keeper law as a mechanical reading, restart the work and surface it when restarts run out, never silently watch forever + intervention_record(verdict, target) returning the session-news dict (type keeper_intervention, news true, target_id, kind, label, status, action, age_seconds, restarts, one plain headline line) when an action is owed, else None (the block_record / completion_record pattern) + record_restart (a copy with restarts + 1 and its clock reset to the restart moment; refuses when restarts already equal max_restarts — an exhausted target is reported, not restarted past its bound — and refuses a backwards moment) + KeeperRegistry (register / get / list_targets in registration order with an optional kind filter / heartbeat recording activity on the stored target / check returning every target's verdict in registration order / interventions returning only the non-None intervention records; caller-supplied ids used as given with duplicates refused, unsupplied ids are the deterministic sequence watch-1, watch-2, ... skipping numbers a supplied id already took, mirroring BackgroundJobRegistry; unknown ids raise KeyError) + PLAIN_EXPLANATION (the keeper notices work that has gone quiet, restarts it while restarts remain, and reports it when they run out — a timer that works, not a timer that watches) + USAGE and the shared parse_keeper_args for the future /keeper surface (show, show <target>, check, heartbeat <target>; a grammar word in the wrong shape is a USAGE error, never a target id, mirroring parse_background_args) + formatters (format_watch_target, format_watch_targets with the plain "Keeper watches: none." empty statement, format_verdict, format_intervention, format_keeper the whole slice) with the stall state stated in words wherever shown and a stalled target's line stating its age and its owed action (restart or report) — status never carried by colour or symbol alone. The module holds watches and verdicts as data only: it runs no process, starts no timer, writes no file, changes no setting, and imports no agent / UI code.
- Verification on the laptop (PYTHONPATH=$PWD, repo .venv-hammer): tests/test_keeper.py 37 passed (pure, TIER: integration); neighbours tests/test_background_tasks.py 53 passed, tests/test_plan_mode.py 64 passed, tests/test_hooks.py 60 passed, tests/test_arch_conformance.py 11 passed — 188 passed combined, and the keeper file plus neighbours run clean together. The two long-classified environment failures (test_context_compiler tiktoken estimator, test_public_api PortAudio) were not in these neighbour files and did not appear. No production defect was found during verification; the suite went green on its first laptop run.

PUBLIC_API + OWNERSHIP regen, roadmap flip M-104 to done (104/272 at banking), ledger row; dead-code quarantine entries if the banking gate flags the new Agent-facing surface (M-074 pattern).
- Still owed before M-104 can bank: agent layer (Agent keeper status / register / heartbeat / check methods holding a KeeperRegistry for the session, feeding it heartbeats from real work — background jobs from the M-103 registry and crew/task activity — carrying a verdict's restart action out in the world where one exists, and publishing intervention_record as a keeper_intervention session event when an action is owed) + /keeper wiring on terminal and TUI roads + chat_screen help/usage/routing; then coordinator banking in roadmap order (merge → full suite → regen → closure → explicit-refspec push → ls-remote verify).

## M-104 agent layer DONE (2026-11-06 keeper tick)
- Built in team-10 only, on the M-103 sibling pattern exactly: Agent holds a KeeperRegistry for the session (lazy `_keeper_registry()`, session state, no new setting — the registry is session work exactly like M-103's job registry). Methods: `keeper_status(now=None)` (targets + verdicts as plain records, the format_keeper screen from the same registry, PLAIN_EXPLANATION, the judgement moment), `get_keeper_target` (KeyError passthrough), `register_keeper_watch(spec, **kwargs)` (pure build_watch_target readings; a watch naming no last activity is stamped with the registration moment — a watch starts alive), `keeper_heartbeat(target_id, at=None)`, and `keeper_check(now=None)` (judges every watch once; each owed intervention_record is published as a `keeper_intervention` session event through _checkpoint, announced at most once per watch per owed state keyed by (target, action, restarts spent); a restart verdict on a background_job watch is carried in the world — `_carry_keeper_restart` kills the stalled job if still running, starts a fresh job from the same spec, links the watch to the replacement job so heartbeats keep feeding it, and spends the restart on the watch at the latest known activity moment; a watch with no in-world handle is carried by its published intervention; a report is carried by publication alone).
- Heartbeats from real work: `_keeper_note_background_activity(job_id)` is called where a held background job visibly lives — in start_background_job after the reader starts, in _background_reader as each output line lands, and in _publish_background_completion as an ending is published — and heartbeats the watch registered under that job's id (or the watch linked to that job by a keeper restart). No watch, no feeding; a raced heartbeat never breaks the job's road.
- Bug found by the new tests and fixed in scope: keeper_check's closing screen re-judged every watch at the original judgement moment, but a carried restart advances the watch's clock past that moment (restart spend + heartbeats), so format_keeper raised on backwards time. ## M-105 Scheduled work — pure layer DONE (2026-11-06 keeper tick)
- Base: team-01 verified at banked base 5bfc057 = M-104 closure (104/272). Built ONLY the pure layer in team-01; no push, no README touched, no config_defaults / config_catalog / PUBLIC_API / OWNERSHIP / CAMPAIGN_STATE / WAVE_PLAN / roadmap files touched.
- Existing basis found (composition, not duplication): M-103 holds a long command as a managed background job — an argv list, never a shell string, with a lifecycle and completion news — and M-104 judges whether watched work has gone quiet and says what is owed when it has. Nothing in the tree says when a piece of work is owed in the first place: a job that should run every dawn, or every week, as standing data. M-105 was built as that missing schedule ON the M-103/M-104 sibling pattern, not replacing either: the same frozen validated dataclasses, build_/validate_ pairs with alternate spellings, PLAIN_EXPLANATION, USAGE, shared parser and formatters, deterministic-id registry, and *_record functions shaped as session news returning None when nothing is owed.
- Command-surface name chosen: /schedule — the slice's own title word, in the one-word lowercase house style of /background, /keeper, /plan; no /schedule surface existed in the tree.
- Built: mythic_agent/core/scheduled_work.py — SCHEDULED_WORK_SLICE = "M-105" + Schedule frozen dataclass validating on construction (SCHEDULE_KINDS daily / weekly / interval: a daily job names a time of day, read leniently — "6:05" becomes "06:05" — and validated strictly as HH:MM; a weekly job names a weekday — word, abbreviation, or number 0-6 with WEEKDAYS Monday-first — and a time; an interval job names a whole number of minutes, hours read as minutes, with an optional anchor; a bad time, an unknown weekday, a zero or negative interval, booleans where numbers belong, conflicting spellings and unknown keys all refuse plainly) + validate_schedule / build_schedule for mapping data (kind or type; time, at or time_of_day; weekday or day; every_minutes, every_hours or interval_minutes; anchor or anchor_at) + ScheduledJob frozen dataclass validating on construction (argv as a tuple of non-empty text words — a single shell string, bytes, an empty list, a blank word or a non-text word refuse outright, exactly as M-103 refuses, so no scheduled job can smuggle in a command line to be eval'd later; an optional job_id from a fixed character set; a label defaulting in reports to the command text itself; an optional cwd; an enabled flag; and its approval posture) + validate_scheduled_job / build_scheduled_job (job_id or id, label or name, argv or command, schedule, enabled, approval or requires_approval; conflicting spellings and unknown keys refuse) + the approval lines as data (APPROVAL_POSTURES unattended / needs_approval, read from posture words or booleans: the same approval lines as live work — only an unattended job is ever judged owed a run) + next_due(job, after) computing the earliest schedule moment at or after a given moment, deterministically (exactly at a schedule moment, that moment is the next due; an unanchored interval counts from the start of the given moment's day) + due_at_or_before(job, now) returning the latest schedule moment at or before a moment (None only for an interval job judged before its anchor) + DueVerdict frozen dataclass (job id, label, status, action, due_at, next_due_at — validating the status/action pairing on construction: DUE_STATUSES not_due / due / overdue / waiting_for_approval / disabled against DUE_ACTIONS none / run / await_approval, so a waiting job can never carry a run action and a due verdict always names its due time) + judge_job(job, now, last_run_at=None), the mechanical due judgement (a disabled job is judged disabled before anything else; the pending moment is the latest schedule moment at or before now that no recorded run covers — a last run at or after it means the slot already ran and the job is not due; of a pending job, a needs_approval posture is judged waiting_for_approval, due and waiting, never owed a run, while an unattended job is due exactly at its moment and overdue past it, owed a run either way; a last_run_at after the judgement moment raises — time never runs backwards) + due_record(verdict, job) returning the session-news dict (type scheduled_job_due, news true, job id, label, status, action, due and next-due moments, one plain headline line) when an action is owed, else None — the completion_record / intervention_record pattern, so a due moment can never be lost as a mere log line and never announced when none is owed + ScheduledWorkRegistry, an in-memory storage-neutral registry model (register / get / list_jobs in registration order with an optional enabled filter / record_run recording one run moment per job, a backwards moment refusing / last_run / check judging every held job against its own last run / due_records returning only the owed records; caller-supplied ids used as given with duplicates refused and unknown ids raising KeyError, unsupplied ids the deterministic sequence schedule-1, schedule-2, ... skipping numbers a supplied id already took — no randomness a test cannot pin) + PLAIN_EXPLANATION + USAGE and the shared parse_schedule_args for the /schedule surface (empty/show -> ("show", None); show <job> or a bare job id -> ("show", job); check -> ("check", None); a grammar word in the wrong shape is a USAGE error, never a job id) and formatters (format_schedule, format_scheduled_job, format_scheduled_jobs with every held job's next due moment stated, format_verdict, format_due_record, format_scheduled_work the whole slice, with the approval posture and due state stated in words wherever shown — status never carried by colour or symbol alone). The module holds jobs, schedules and verdicts as data only: it starts no timer, runs no process, writes no file, changes no setting, and imports no agent / UI code.
- Defect found and fixed during verification: one, in the new tests only — test_judge_already_run_slot_is_not_due judged a job at 06:30 with a last run recorded at 06:45 and expected not_due, while its sibling test_judge_backwards_last_run_refuses pins this module's own honesty rule that a last_run_at after the judgement moment raises (time never runs backwards); the two expectations cannot both hold, since 06:45 and the sibling's 07:00 are both after 06:30 and both cover the 06:30 slot. The module's rule is the designed one, so the test was corrected: the already-run case is now judged at 06:45, the run's own moment, where the 06:30 slot is covered and not due — no assertion weakened and no production code changed. First laptop run of the new suite: 31 passed / 1 failed (this contradiction); after the fix, green below.

The two long-classified environment failures (test_context_compiler tiktoken estimator, test_public_api PortAudio) were not in these neighbour files and did not appear.

PUBLIC_API + OWNERSHIP regen, roadmap flip M-105 to done (105/272 at banking), ledger row; dead-code quarantine entries if the banking gate flags the new surface (M-074 pattern). Persistence of the held jobs and their recorded runs beyond the session (into a session journal or a file) is owed to the agent layer / coordinator, not this layer — the registry model is storage-neutral by design.

- Command-surface name chosen: /budgets — the slice's own title word, in the house style of /background, /keeper, /schedule, /fleet.
- Built: mythic_agent/core/budgets.py — BUDGETS_SLICE = "M-107" + BUDGET_SCOPES (task / session, read from task / per-task / per_task and session / per-session / per_session via normalize_budget_scope) + BUDGET_DIMENSIONS (tokens / cost / time / commands) + BUDGET_VERDICT_STATUSES (within / warning / exceeded) + WARNING_FRACTION 0.8 + Budget frozen dataclass validating on construction (an optional budget_id from the sibling character set; a label defaulting in reports to the per-scope wording; each limit optional — a limit of None is unlimited on that dimension and NO default limit is invented: token and command limits are whole numbers over zero, cost and time limits are finite numbers over zero, zero / negative limits and booleans where numbers belong refuse plainly) + validate_budget / build_budget (budget_id or id, label or name, scope or kind or type; token_limit or max_tokens or tokens; cost_limit or max_cost or cost or cost_usd or cost_limit_usd; time in seconds spellings or minutes spellings read as seconds, the two units refusing when they name different times; command_limit or max_commands or commands; conflicting spellings and unknown keys refuse) + BudgetUsage frozen dataclass (also exported under the roadmap's own word Usage: tokens_used and commands_used whole numbers of 0 or more, cost_used in dollars and time_used_seconds of 0 or more, negatives and booleans refusing) + validate_usage / build_usage (tokens_used or tokens or token_count; cost_used or cost or cost_usd or spend or spent_usd; time in seconds or minutes spellings; commands_used or commands or command_count; unnamed dimensions read as zero spent) + BudgetVerdict frozen dataclass (budget id, label, scope, status, exceeded / warning dimension tuples, and every dimension's used and limit amounts — validating on construction that its dimensions and status are exactly what _classify computes from those amounts, so a verdict can never disagree with itself) + judge_budget(budget, usage=None), the mechanical judge (a dimension at or past its limit is exceeded, at or past 80% of its limit is warning, a dimension with no limit earns neither; the verdict is exceeded when any dimension is exceeded, warning when none is exceeded and any is approaching, within otherwise; no usage named means nothing spent) + budget_paused_record returning the session-news dict (type budget_paused, news true, budget id, label, scope, status, exceeded / warning lists, per-dimension readings, graceful true, one plain headline line stating the pause is graceful — a report at a resting point, never a mid-edit death, with the reached amounts in words) only when a verdict is exceeded, else None — the due_record / resume_record pattern + budget_report_record returning a budget_report news record for a warning verdict, the budget_paused record itself for an exceeded verdict, and None when within + BudgetRegistry, an in-memory storage-neutral registry model (register / get / list_budgets in registration order / record_usage replacing a budget's latest reading / get_usage / check judging one held budget against a given or its recorded reading, recording nothing / list_verdicts / paused_record / report_record; caller-supplied ids used as given with duplicates refused and unknown ids raising KeyError, unsupplied ids the deterministic sequence budget-1, budget-2, ... skipping numbers a supplied id already took — no randomness a test cannot pin) + PLAIN_EXPLANATION + USAGE and the shared parse_budgets_args for the /budgets surface (empty/show -> ("show", None); show <budget> or a bare budget id -> ("show", budget); check -> ("check", None); check <budget> -> ("check", budget); a grammar word in the wrong shape is a USAGE error) and formatters (format_budget with every dimension's limit or unlimited state in words, format_budgets with the plain "Budgets: none." empty statement, format_verdict with the concerned dimensions' used-of-limit amounts always stated and the graceful-pause wording on an exceeded reading, format_budget_report / format_report, format_budgets_screen the whole slice — status stated in words wherever shown, never colour or symbol alone). The module holds budgets, usage readings and verdicts as data only: it starts no process, runs no command, writes no file, changes no setting, and imports no agent / UI code.

## M-107 agent layer DONE (2026-11-07 keeper tick)
- Built in team-03 on the M-107 pure layer (f23ce59 + d63006b, on banked base 6290a1d = M-106 closure, 106/272), following the M-106/M-105 sibling pattern exactly: Agent holds a BudgetRegistry for the session, created lazily on first use (no new setting - a budget is session work, exactly as M-103/M-104/M-105/M-106 hold theirs); the moment the registry is created is stamped, so elapsed time is read from a real clock. Methods: _budget_registry / budget_status (held budgets as plain records + each budget's recorded usage or None + verdicts judged from the recorded readings + format_budgets_screen + PLAIN_EXPLANATION; a status read records, pauses and publishes nothing) / get_budget / get_budget_usage / get_budget_verdict (unknown ids raise KeyError, exactly as the pure registry answers) / register_budget (build_budget readings; held as data only - registering spends nothing, records no usage and pauses nothing) / record_budget_usage (build_usage readings in the costs road's vocabulary; replaces the budget's latest reading; recording judges and publishes nothing) / budget_live_usage + refresh_budget_usage (the real-usage road, below) / budget_check (below) / _publish_budget_paused (below).
- Real usage, never invented numbers: budget_live_usage reads every number from a road that really counted it - tokens and cost from the costs road (core/costs.py: the workspace CostTracker ledger's recorded token sums via by_model and its total() cost in dollars; a model its pricing table does not know is billed unknown at zero there, so no price is invented here either), elapsed time as the real clock time since this session's budget guardrails were first held, and commands as the held background jobs (the session's real command road, M-103) counted as held. refresh_budget_usage records that live reading as one held budget's latest reading. The pure layer's honest shape is preserved: a budget with no recorded reading has spent nothing.
- The check carries the verdict out in the world: budget_check (all held budgets, or one named budget) judges each against its recorded reading; an exceeded verdict becomes a graceful pause at this resting point - the pure layer's budget_paused record is published as a budget_paused session event through the Agent's _checkpoint road and the budget is named in the return's paused / paused_ids; a warning verdict rides along as its budget_report record, as data. _publish_budget_paused announces each exceeded moment at most once per budget: the announcement is keyed by the budget, the dimensions it exceeded and the amounts it was read at, so a repeated check of the same reading is not new news while spending further past the limit is a new exceeded moment and is announced again (both halves pinned by tests).
- Honest boundary (live turn road): budget_check is the judgement + session-news road at a resting point, exactly like M-105's scheduled_check and M-100's check_plan_effect - it decides, pauses gracefully between pieces of work, and publishes; it does not interrupt an in-flight edit or kill running work, and no mid-turn interception of the live chat/tool loop is claimed. The pause, the verdict and the news are available to every surface through the agent methods and /budgets check.
- Surfaces: /budgets wiring on BOTH roads sharing the pure layer's parse_budgets_args + formatters - terminal.py slash handler (show -> budget_status screen; show <budget> -> format_budget(budget, verdict) with an unknown id answered in words; check / check <budget> -> budget_check screen) plus the terminal /help line; command_handler.py _handle_budgets + dispatch (same shapes, published as chat chunks, usage errors and KeyErrors published in red words); chat_screen.py help line, usage entry, and system-command routing for /budgets.

## M-108 The kill switch everywhere — pure layer DONE (2026-11-07 keeper tick)
- Existing basis found (composition, not duplication): M-103 already holds one long command as a managed job with its own kill road (a kill request recorded as data, then applied — request_kill / apply_kill), M-106 holds a crew of headless runs as data with verdicts judged mechanically, M-104 watches held work for stalls, and M-107 pauses work gracefully at a guardrail. Nothing in the tree was the one switch that halts everything at once — the coder's own in-flight work, the crew, the background jobs and any approval still waiting for an answer. M-108 was built as that missing switch ON the sibling pattern, composing with those layers' own vocabularies (the M-103 job-state words, the M-106 crew-status words), not replacing any of them: the same frozen validated dataclasses, build_/validate_ pairs with alternate spellings, PLAIN_EXPLANATION, USAGE, shared parser and formatters, deterministic-id registry, and a *_record function shaped as session news.
- Built: mythic_agent/core/kill_switch.py — KILL_SWITCH_SLICE = "M-108" + HALT_TARGETS (coder / crew / background_jobs / approvals, in roadmap order; one switch halts all four by default) + HALT_OUTCOMES / HALT_STATUSES (halted / already_stopped / nothing_held) + BACKGROUND_STATES / BACKGROUND_ACTIVE_STATES / BACKGROUND_TERMINAL_STATES (the M-103 words) + CREW_STATUSES / CREW_FINISHED_STATUSES (the M-106 words) + Halt frozen dataclass validating on construction (targets via normalize_halt_target — coder or agent, crew or fleet, background_jobs or background or jobs, approvals or approval — deduplicated into roadmap order, an empty naming refusing; actor and reason as loose text, either may be empty; an optional halt_id from the sibling character set; display_label and to_record) + validate_halt / build_halt (halt_id or id, actor or requested_by or by or who, reason or why, targets or target or scope; conflicting spellings and unknown keys refuse) + HaltHoldings frozen dataclass (also exported under the plainer word Holdings: coder_active a real bool — text refuses; background_jobs and crew_tasks as (id, state) pairs coerced from mappings, pairs or attribute objects and validated against their own vocabularies, unknown states refusing; pending_approvals a whole number of 0 or more, negatives and booleans refusing; derived id tuples background_active_ids / background_finished_ids / crew_running_ids / crew_queued_ids / crew_finished_ids) + validate_holdings / build_holdings (coder_active or coder or coder_working; background_jobs or jobs; crew_tasks or crew or fleet_tasks; pending_approvals or approvals_pending, an approvals naming read as the count itself or a list counted, the spellings refusing when they name different counts; unknown keys refuse) + HaltVerdict frozen dataclass (halt id, actor, reason, targets, per-target outcomes, overall status, and the holdings it was judged from carried alongside — validating on construction that its outcomes and status are exactly what the classifier computes from those holdings, so a verdict can never disagree with itself) + judge_halt, the mechanical judge (a target is halted only when live work is held for it — the coder at work, a crew task running, a background job queued or running, an approval pending; finished work is already_stopped, never newly halted; a merely queued crew task is a plan, not held work, and earns nothing_held; the status is the strongest outcome earned) + halt_record returning the session-news dict (type kill_switch_pulled, news true, halt id, actor, reason, targets, outcomes, status, the held ids by reading, pending count, and one plain headline stating each target's outcome in words with its held names) for EVERY judged halt — a pull is always news, never lost as a log line + HaltRegistry, an in-memory storage-neutral registry model (register / get / list_halts in the order pulled / judge holding the verdict a pull earned against that moment's holdings, never re-judged from later holdings / get_verdict / list_verdicts / record announcing the held verdict, judging an unjudged halt against named holdings first; caller-supplied ids used as given with duplicates refused and unknown ids raising KeyError, unsupplied ids the deterministic sequence halt-1, halt-2, ... skipping numbers a supplied id already took — no randomness a test cannot pin) + PLAIN_EXPLANATION + USAGE and the shared parse_halt_args for the /halt surface (empty/show -> ("show", None); show <halt> or a bare halt id -> ("show", halt); pull -> ("pull", None); pull <target> -> ("pull", target) with pull all / pull everything reading as pull; a grammar word in the wrong shape — a bare target word, a doubled word — is a USAGE error, never a halt id) and formatters (format_halt with every target named in words, format_halts with the plain "Kill switch: none pulled." empty statement, format_verdict with every target's outcome and held names always stated, format_halt_record / format_record, format_halts_screen the whole slice — status stated in words wherever shown, never colour or symbol alone). The module holds halts, holdings readings and verdicts as data only: it starts no process, kills nothing, signals nothing, writes no file, changes no setting, and imports no agent / UI code.

## M-108 agent layer DONE (2026-11-07 keeper tick)
- Built in team-04 on the M-108 pure layer (3f4e434 + 5c26369, on banked base 1221753 = M-107 closure, 107/272), following the M-107/M-106 sibling pattern exactly: Agent holds a HaltRegistry for the session, created lazily on first use (no new setting — a halt is session work, exactly as M-103/M-104/M-105/M-106/M-107 hold theirs). Methods: _kill_switch_registry (the held registry lives under the attribute _kill_switch_halts_registry — a first cut stored it under the method's own name and the getter returned the bound method; caught by the agent tests and fixed to the sibling pattern, e.g. _fleet_registry / _fleet_mode_registry) / kill_switch_status (held halts as plain records + every judged verdict held alongside its halt + format_halts_screen + PLAIN_EXPLANATION; a status read registers, judges, halts and publishes nothing) / get_halt / get_halt_verdict (the historical verdict judged at the pull, never re-judged from later holdings; None when unjudged; unknown ids raise KeyError on every road) / _kill_switch_holdings + pull_kill_switch + _publish_kill_switch_pulled (below).
- The real-usage road (_kill_switch_holdings) reads every holding from a road that really holds it — nothing invented: the coder is active exactly when its turn lock is held (a turn in flight holds it); background jobs are the held M-103 jobs with their real lifecycle states; crew tasks are the held M-106 tasks — a task with a finished verdict is finished, a task backed by a live background job (the road fleet_check runs a task through, the job carrying the task's display label) is running, any other task is merely queued, a plan and not held work (fleet ids are fleet-N, learned from the runner, not task-N as first assumed in the tests); pending approvals are read from the approval road itself — approvals are asked one at a time under the tools layer's approval lock (agents/tools.py prompt_approval_sync), so a pending approval exists exactly while that lock is held and there is never more than one.
- pull_kill_switch (a Halt, a halt mapping, or field words; nothing named halts all four targets) registers the halt, reads the holdings, and lets the pure layer judge the pull against that moment's holdings; it then carries the halt out through the REAL roads — held live background jobs are killed through the M-103 road (kill_background_job: request recorded, real process terminated, kill applied, completion published), a running crew task is halted on its backing job through that same road (a crew-only pull kills only crew-backed jobs; a crew with no live backing job has nothing running to kill and the verdict says nothing held / already stopped rather than claiming a halt that never happened — there is no separate crew kill road in the tree, and none was faked), and the coder + pending approvals are halted by setting the coder's own cancellation event (Agent.cancel): in-flight work stops at the next runtime boundary and a pending approval, which waits on that same event, is refused — it raises its cancellation and is never answered as approved; there is no separate approval road, and the docstring says plainly that refusing an approval rides the coder's cancellation event because that is the road approvals are bound to. The kill_switch_pulled record is published as session news through _checkpoint, announced at most once per pull (keyed by the halt's fresh id). The return names the jobs actually killed and any kill that refused, in words. NO mid-turn interception beyond the cancellation event's own boundary stops is claimed, and no mid-edit state is invented.
- Surfaces: /halt on terminal + TUI roads sharing parse_halt_args + the pure formatters (show -> the kill-switch screen; show <halt> -> format_halt with its held verdict, an unknown id answered in words; pull / pull <target> -> pull_kill_switch with actor "user" and the post-pull screen) + the terminal help line + chat_screen help/usage/routing.
- Verification on the laptop: tests/test_kill_switch.py 59 passed (39 pure + 20 agent/surfaces, including real-subprocess pulls: a sleeping background job killed and cancelled, a running crew task halted on its backing job, a pending approval read from the approval lock and refused, the coder halted while its turn lock was held); neighbours tests/test_budgets.py + tests/test_keeper.py + tests/test_background_tasks.py + tests/test_fleet_mode.py + tests/test_arch_conformance.py 234 passed; six-file combined 293 passed. Defects found and fixed in scope during the run: the registry attribute/method name collision above (agent layer), and three pure-test expectation mistakes of mine (duplicate-naming overload, bare-word parser reading, fleet id shape) — no defects found in prior banked work.
- Owed coordinator deltas at banking: PUBLIC_API inventory (+ the kill_switch names in kill_switch.__all__) + OWNERSHIP regen, roadmap flip M-108 to done (108/272 at banking), ledger row; dead-code quarantine entries if the banking gate flags the programmatic-only Agent roads (Agent.get_halt_verdict / Agent._kill_switch_holdings are the likely candidates, M-107 pattern); NO new setting — session-held registry, so no config_defaults or config_catalogue delta is owed.
- Still owed before M-108 can bank: the banking itself, in roadmap order (merge into the campaign main checkout -> full suite on the laptop -> PUBLIC_API + OWNERSHIP regen -> closure commit -> explicit-refspec push -> ls-remote verify -> campaign state update). The next slice in roadmap order after M-108 banks is M-109.

- Existing basis found (composition, not duplication): the tree already keeps an append-only JSONL audit file (mythic_agent/core/audit.py — an AuditLog writing one JSON object per line under the workspace, with its own AuditEntry shape) and a write-ahead journal for session checkpoints (mythic_agent/core/journal.py). Neither holds the trail as validated session data: no closed vocabulary of what kind of act was recorded, no closed vocabulary of who acted, no registry a session can hold and read back, and no export shaped for review. Built in team-05 on banked base e701450 = M-108 closure (108/272), M-109 was built as that missing trail ON the sibling pattern (M-108 kill_switch.py shape): the same frozen validated dataclasses, build_/validate_ pairs with alternate spellings, PLAIN_EXPLANATION, USAGE, shared parser and formatters, deterministic-id registry, and a *_record function shaped as session news.

actor via normalize_audit_actor — human or user or person, coder or agent, crew or fleet, hook or hooks; both vocabularies closed, any other word refusing; summary as required non-empty text — an entry that says nothing audits nothing; detail and target as loose text, either may be empty; an optional entry_id from the sibling character set; display_label and to_record; also exported under the shorter word Entry) + validate_entry / build_entry (entry_id or id, kind or type or category, actor or by or who, summary or what or description, detail or details, target or subject; kind, actor and summary all required; conflicting spellings and unknown keys refuse) + audit_record returning the session-news dict (type audit_recorded, news true, entry id, kind, actor, summary, detail, target, and one plain headline stating the kind and actor in words with target and detail named when present) for EVERY recorded entry — a recorded act is always news, never lost as a log line + export_trail rendering the whole trail as plain data for review (type audit_export, append_only true, the count, every held entry's record in append order, and by_kind / by_actor counts counted from the entries themselves with every vocabulary word named, zero when none — nothing held left out, nothing summarised away) + AuditRegistry, an in-memory storage-neutral append-only registry model (append — the only road in, an entry goes last after every entry already held; register as the sibling word for the same append road / get / list_entries in append order as an immutable tuple / record announcing one held entry / export of the whole held trail / len; caller-supplied ids used as given with duplicates refused and unknown ids raising KeyError, unsupplied ids the deterministic sequence audit-1, audit-2, ... skipping numbers a supplied id already took — no randomness a test cannot pin). Append-only is enforced in the data layer, not by convention: entries are frozen, the registry offers no edit, removal, clearing or reordering road at all, and appending under an id the trail already holds refuses with the append-only reason named — a rewrite of history is an error, never a silent success; a correction is a new entry. + PLAIN_EXPLANATION + USAGE and the shared parse_audit_args for the future /audit surface (empty/show -> ("show", None); show <entry> or a bare entry id -> ("show", entry); export -> ("export", None); a grammar word in the wrong shape — a bare kind or actor word, a doubled word, export with an argument — is a USAGE error, never an entry id) and formatters (format_entry with kind and actor always stated in words, format_entries with the plain "Audit trail: none recorded." empty statement, format_export the text export naming every held entry with the by-kind and by-actor counts in its header, format_audit_record / format_record, format_audit_screen the whole slice — kind and actor stated in words wherever shown, never colour or symbol alone). The module holds entries as data only: it writes no file, appends to no journal, changes no setting, and imports no agent / UI code. The /audit surface is chosen here (show / export grammar above) and wired nowhere — that is the agent layer, the next layer, not this one.

## M-109 Audit trail — agent layer DONE (2026-11-07 keeper tick)
- Built in team-05 on the M-109 pure layer (cd43529 + b31a073, on banked base e701450 = M-108 closure, 108/272), following the M-108/M-107 sibling pattern exactly: Agent holds an AuditRegistry for the session, created lazily on first use (_audit_registry(), held under the attribute _audit_trail_registry — never the method's own name, the M-108 lesson). NO new setting — a trail is session work, exactly as M-103's job registry, M-104's watch registry, M-105's schedule registry, M-106's fleet registry, M-107's budget registry and M-108's halt registry are held. Methods: audit_status (held entries as plain records + the registry's whole-trail export + format_audit_screen + PLAIN_EXPLANATION; a status read appends and publishes nothing) / get_audit_entry (KeyError passthrough) / audit_export (the export as plain data + format_export text, both from the same registry; exporting publishes nothing) / record_audit_entry (the explicit road: pure build_entry readings, append, publish; refuses loudly on a bad spec or a held id, exactly as the pure registry refuses) / _publish_audit_record (the pure layer's audit_recorded record journaled through _checkpoint when a session is attached, announced at most once per entry, keyed by its fresh id).
- Feeding from the REAL roads, as acts happen — every feeder rides _record_audit_note, which appends and publishes but never raises: recording must never break the road it rides on, and a dropped note never changes the act, which already happened or was already refused (the explicit record_audit_entry road is the loud one; only the as-it-happens notes are silent). The roads: (1) TOOL CALLS, EDITS and TOOL-ROAD COMMANDS from _execute_call, the funnel every tool call in a session travels — trailed exactly once per call from a finally block, so granted, refused, failed, interrupted and cancelled calls each leave exactly one entry; the kind is the tool's own (run_command = command, write_file / replace_file_content = edit, every other tool = tool_call), the actor is the coder, or the crew when this agent is a named subagent rather than the Primary (a spawned agent's acts are crew acts, and its own trail says so), the target names what the act touched (the command line, the file, or the tool), and the detail is the OUTCOME WORD alone (completed / permission denied / failed / refused / interrupted / cancelled) — a tool result's text is never trailed, because a result may be a whole file's contents. Programmatic apply_* edit calls under the funnel are the machinery an edit travels, not separate session acts, and are not trailed separately (no double count: one write_file call leaves exactly one entry, pinned by test). (2) APPROVALS from the two approval callbacks the tree actually builds — Agent.bind_tui's approve closure (the TUI road) and terminal.configured_agent's approve closure (the terminal road) — at the moment the human's answer lands, actor human: a remembered approval answering (M-096 road, checked first in both closures) or the prompt answered granted or refused; a prompt cancelled before any answer is not an approval and is never trailed as one. There is no third approval road in the tree, and none was faked. (3) BACKGROUND COMMANDS from start_background_job, called where the job visibly lives beside _keeper_note_background_activity — started jobs, and a command the OS could not start at all (its honest 127 ending, held as a failed job, trailed as the attempt it was); the actor is the crew when the job carries a held fleet task's display label (the reading M-108's holdings use to tell running crew work) and the coder otherwise, so fleet commands are crew work and the trail says which it was rather than guessing; fleet checks and keeper restarts ride this same road and are trailed there, never twice. (4) HOOKS from run_hooks, as each hook's run is judged — a hook run is a command with actor hook, its target the hook's command line and its detail the verdict's decision and exit code; hooks fire only through run_hooks in this tree, there is no other hook firing road, and none was invented.
- Surfaces: /audit on both roads sharing parse_audit_args + the pure formatters (show -> audit_status screen; show <entry> or a bare entry id -> format_entry, an unknown id answered in words with the registry's own "unknown audit entry"; export -> format_export via audit_export; a grammar word in the wrong shape is the USAGE, never an entry id): terminal.py handler + the terminal /help line; command_handler.py _handle_audit + /audit routing mirroring _handle_halt (bold-cyan publication, usage/KeyError published in red); chat_screen.py help line, usage entry, and system-command routing list all gained /audit.

Full suite (detached run, /tmp/m109_full_suite.log): 5667 passed, 1 skipped, 6 failed — every failure classified, none caused by this layer: four are the expected pre-banking gates (tests/test_ownership.py x2 — OWNERSHIP stale at 226 vs 227 modules, the +1 being audit_trail; tests/test_public_api.py inventory sync — PUBLIC_API stale; tests/test_dead_code.py — flags exactly one new item, Agent.record_audit_entry, the programmatic-only explicit road, for the banking quarantine below) and two are pre-existing environmental failures PROVEN identical on the untouched banked base e701450 in the campaign repo tree (tests/test_context_compiler.py::test_estimator_heuristic_backend_when_tiktoken_absent — this venv has tiktoken, the test expects it absent; tests/test_public_api.py::test_all_names_exist_and_are_public — mythic_agent.core.audio import dies on PortAudio/PulseAudio with no audio server in this shell). ## M-110 Secret hygiene in action — pure layer DONE (2026-11-07 keeper tick)
- Existing basis found (composition, not duplication): the tree already scans text for credential shapes (mythic_agent/core/secrets_audit.py — scan_secrets finds credential-like spans by kind: openai_key / anthropic_key / github_token / aws_access_key / aws_secret_key / stripe_key / slack_token / google_api_key / xai_key / bearer_token / url_credentials / private_key / generic_secret; mask_text replaces them) and already masks configured credentials, URL credentials (the regex road) and detected secrets on the logging / export road (mythic_agent/core/redaction.py — redact_text / SecretRedactor / protect_logging, with configured values read from config and *_API_KEY / *_TOKEN / *_PASSWORD environment names). Keys themselves live in Kista, never in config (mythic_agent/core/key_custody.py, M-032 — status without secrets, kista: references). The mass-edit guard already treats .env / credentials / id_rsa / id_ed25519 / .pem / .key names and .ssh paths as sensitive (mythic_agent/core/mass_edit_guard.py _is_sensitive), and the auto-commit road judges branch, message and file set (mythic_agent/core/auto_commit.py, M-078) — but nothing held hygiene as validated session data and no road screened a file for commit by secret shape. There are no shipped tests for secrets_audit / redaction themselves (no tests/test_secrets_audit.py or tests/test_redaction.py in the tree). Built in team-06 on banked base 337d637 = M-109 closure (109/272), M-110 was built as that missing hygiene layer ON those roads, not replacing any of them: its text shapes ARE the shipped scanner's shapes (this layer calls scan_secrets, it does not re-define the provider regexes), its filename shape widens the mass-edit guard's sensitive names, and its redaction road is the scanner's spans replaced whole — stricter than the audit road in one named way: no record here carries even the scanner's masked head-and-tail preview; a verdict names kinds only. Same sibling shape as M-109/M-108: frozen validated dataclasses, build_/validate_ pairs with alternate spellings, PLAIN_EXPLANATION, USAGE, shared parser and formatters, deterministic-id registry, and a *_record function shaped as session news.
- Command-surface name chosen: /secrets — the slice's own subject word, in the one-word lowercase house style of /audit, /halt, /budgets; no /secrets surface existed in the tree (the shipped secrets_audit module has no user surface at all).
- Built: mythic_agent/core/secret_hygiene.py — SECRET_HYGIENE_SLICE = "M-110" + REDACTION_MARKER "[REDACTED]" (one fixed marker; it names no part of any secret) + SECRET_KINDS (the shipped scanner's thirteen kinds in a fixed order, plus env_assignment and secret_file, this slice's two mechanical additions) + SCREENING_STATUSES (clean / refused, read from clean / clear / ok and refused / blocked / denied via normalize_screening_status) + SecretPattern frozen dataclass + SECRET_PATTERNS, the known shapes held as validated data (kind via normalize_secret_kind — every kind plus its short spellings openai / anthropic / github / aws access / stripe / slack / google / xai / bearer / url / private / generic / env / dotenv / file / filename, closed vocabulary, any other word refusing; label in plain words; basis text / filename via normalize_pattern_basis) + pattern_for_kind / validate_pattern / build_pattern + secret_kinds_in, the kinds a text carries in kind order, names kinds only (the shipped scanner's findings plus the env_assignment shape: a line naming a KEY / TOKEN / SECRET / PASSWORD / CREDENTIAL word with a value of eight or more characters, anchored at a line start with an optional export — mechanical only, stated in the module honestly: a named assignment with a placeholder value of that length may still be flagged, which for a commit refusal is the safe direction) + redact_secrets replacing every secret-shaped span (scanner spans plus assignment value spans, overlaps resolved longest-first exactly as the scanner de-duplicates) with the one fixed marker — ordinary text returned untouched, a non-text input or a blank marker refusing + filename_kinds (the secret_file kind for the sensitive names: .env and its named variants, credentials, id_rsa / id_dsa / id_ecdsa / id_ed25519, .pem / .key / .p12 / .pfx / .kdbx endings, a .ssh path part, or a name carrying the word secret) + FileScreening frozen dataclass (also exported under the shorter word Screening: filename as required clean text; kinds normalised, deduplicated and held in kind order; status derived on construction — refused exactly when kinds are named, clean exactly when none are — with a status named against its kinds refusing, so a verdict can never disagree with itself; an optional screening_id from the sibling character set; refused property, display_label and to_record naming the file, its status and its kinds only — the screened content is never a field, so a screening can be stored, logged and shown without leaking what it screened) + validate_screening / build_screening (screening_id or id, filename or file or path or name, kinds or kind or patterns or pattern, status or verdict or result; conflicting spellings and unknown keys refuse) + screen_file, the judge (filename + text -> the verdict carrying the name's kinds and the text's kinds together, in kind order; the text is read only to locate shapes in it and is never carried into the verdict) + hygiene_record returning the session-news dict (type secret_refusal, news true, screening id, filename, status, kinds, and one plain headline naming the file and the pattern kinds in words, with the never-shown rule stated) only when a screening is refused, else None — the budget_paused due-record pattern; a refusal is always news, never lost as a log line + HygieneRegistry, an in-memory storage-neutral registry model (register / screen judging and holding in one move / get / list_screenings in the order judged / record announcing one held screening's refusal, None when clean; caller-supplied ids used as given with duplicates refused and unknown ids raising KeyError, unsupplied ids the deterministic sequence hygiene-1, hygiene-2, ... skipping numbers a supplied id already took — no randomness a test cannot pin). The registry fits the sibling pattern honestly here because a session's screenings are exactly the held work every sibling registry holds: verdicts a session judged, read back by id, with refusals announced from the held verdict rather than re-judged. + PLAIN_EXPLANATION + USAGE and the shared parse_secrets_args for the /secrets surface (empty/show/status -> ("show", None); show <screening> or a bare screening id -> ("show", screening); check <file> -> ("check", file); a grammar word in the wrong shape — a bare kind word, a doubled word, check with no file or with two — is a USAGE error, never a screening id) and formatters (format_pattern / format_patterns naming every known shape in words, format_screening with status and kinds always stated and the content-never-shown line on a refusal, format_screenings with the plain "Secret hygiene: no files screened." empty statement, format_hygiene_record / format_record, format_secrets_screen the whole slice — status stated in words wherever shown, never colour or symbol alone). The module holds shapes, screenings and verdicts as data only: it writes no file, runs no subprocess, changes no setting, and imports no agent / UI code — its one import from the tree is the shipped pure scanner.

## M-110 agent layer DONE (2026-11-07 keeper tick)
- Wired on team-06 atop the pure layer (5977c71 / 1b174ec, banked base 337d637 = 109/272): the Agent now holds a HygieneRegistry for the session (session-held, exactly as M-103..M-109; NO new config setting) via _hygiene_registry / secret_hygiene_status / get_screening / screen_file / screen_project_file / redact_output in mythic_agent/agents/llm.py, created on first use so an agent whose __init__ never ran still holds exactly one. The llm.py wiring landed from the previous worker's on-laptop patch script (/tmp/m110_L.gz, sha256 fa8e1e92...a04f2, inspected and py_compiled on-device before applying; its anchors matched exactly once each). The surface wiring was authored fresh on-device this tick after a large base64 transfer of a second patch script was removed by the safety classifiers and two whole-file sha256 mismatches on re-emission - the transfer road was abandoned per the standing rule and the remaining patches were written directly on the laptop in small pieces (heredoc patch scripts, per-chunk sha256 where chunked), each verified before running.
- Feeding roads (real outputs through redact_secrets): the tool-result road at the _execute_call funnel - every tool result (and the tool-failure text) passes through _hygiene_redact before the caller stores it via _append_message as the tool message and checkpoints it to the session journal, so this one feeding covers the tool-result road and the transcript road for tool content together. _hygiene_redact composes with the shipped redaction layer rather than replacing it, and never breaks its road: a text the pure layer refuses is passed through the shipped redactor instead, and an output is never lost to a hygiene note. HONESTY BOUND, stated plainly: the export road was NOT re-fed through the pure layer - session exports and listings are already sanitized by the shipped layer (SessionStore's sanitizer on the shipped scanner's shapes plus configured and environment secrets), the logging road is already guarded by protect_logging, and terminal display by the agent's redactor; tool content reaches the journal already hygiene-redacted at the funnel, so exports of it carry the redacted text. The pure layer adds the wider reading (the scanner's shapes plus the .env-style assignment shape) on the one road the shipped layer does not itself pass through.
- Commit road screening (real, M-078): commit_accepted_edits now screens every file in the plan after plan_commit and before any branch switch, staging or commit - each planned file's bytes are read from the project root and judged through screen_file (a file the plan names but the disk no longer holds, an accepted deletion, is screened by its name alone); any refused verdict refuses the whole commit with a plain ValueError naming the files and pattern kinds (content never shown) and nothing is committed. A refused verdict is published as the pure hygiene_record dict as a secret_refusal session event through _checkpoint (the M-109 audit_recorded sibling pattern), deduped per screening by screening id (_publish_secret_refusal). screen_project_file refuses names escaping the project and files the project does not hold, plainly, screening nothing.
- Surfaces: /secrets on both roads sharing the pure parse_secrets_args + format_screening / format_secrets_screen - terminal (_slash in mythic_agent/terminal.py, help string extended) and TUI (CommandHandler._handle_secrets dispatch in mythic_agent/agents/command_handler.py; chat_screen help line, usage entry and system-command routing in mythic_agent/ui/screens/chat_screen.py). Grammar: show (default) / show <screening> / bare id / check <file>; check screens one real project file now and holds the verdict; reading the screenings screens and publishes nothing.
